# Research Group subset-b-000594

Grouped research for ceph-client Linux networking Devicetree binding YAML schemas covering Ethernet MACs, PHYs, MDIO buses and muxes, switches, connectors, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, MCTP, and PCS bindings. Each source file section is delimited for deterministic splitting into source-tree-aligned per-file research documents.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/engleder,tsnep.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/engleder,tsnep.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `TSN endpoint Ethernet MAC`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `engleder,tsnep`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `dma-coherent`, `local-mac-address`, `mac-address`, `nvmem-cells`, `nvmem-cell-names`, `phy-connection-type`, `phy-mode`, `phy-handle`, `mdio`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=8`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `dma-coherent` integrates the device with SoC PM, DMA, or IOMMU topology; `phy-mode` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure; `phy-handle` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure; `mdio` models MDIO management bus topology. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Gerhard Engleder <gerhard@engleder-embedded.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `ethernet-controller.yaml#`, `mdio.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/engleder/tsnep_main.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 13 top-level properties and 13 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/engleder,tsnep.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/engleder,tsnep.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/engleder,tsnep.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ethernet-phy-package.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ethernet-phy-package.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Ethernet PHY Package Common Properties`, a Ethernet PHY binding. The schema description says: PHY packages are multi-port Ethernet PHY of the same family and each Ethernet PHY is affected by the global configuration of the PHY package. Each reg of the PHYs defined in the PHY package node is absolute and describe the real address of the Ethernet PHY on the MDIO bus. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a composed compatible schema with 0 tokens: no explicit compatible constants. Patterned child schemas are `^ethernet-phy@[a-f0-9]+$`. Numeric and collection constraints include `minimum=0`, `maximum=31`. Important resource roles: `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `#address-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `#size-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape. The highest-risk ABI details are PHY `reg` addresses, compatible fallbacks, reset/interrupt timing, RGMII delays, LED definitions, package topology, and vendor tuning flags.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PHY link autonegotiation, EEE, cable diagnostics, interrupt state, reset state, per-port LEDs, and package shared state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Christian Marangi <ansuelsmth@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `ethernet-phy.yaml#`. Integration points include phylib, phylink, MDIO child nodes, MAC `phy-handle` users, LED subnodes, interrupts, reset GPIOs, regulators, and board-specific strap properties. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. This schema allows unknown top-level properties. It has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ethernet-phy-package.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ethernet-phy-package.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ethernet-phy.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ethernet-phy.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Ethernet PHY Common Properties`, a Ethernet PHY binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 5 branchs with 2 tokens: `ethernet-phy-ieee802.3-c22`, `ethernet-phy-ieee802.3-c45`. Top-level properties are `$nodename`, `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `max-speed`, `phy-10base-t1l-2.4vpp`, `broken-turn-around`, `brr-mode`, `clocks`, `enet-phy-lane-swap`, `enet-phy-lane-no-swap`, `enet-phy-pair-order`, `enet-phy-pair-polarity`, `eee-broken-100tx`, `eee-broken-1000t`, `eee-broken-10gt`, `eee-broken-1000kx`, `eee-broken-10gkx4`, `eee-broken-10gkr`, `timing-role`, `pses`, `phy-is-integrated`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `reset-gpios`, `reset-assert-us`, `reset-deassert-us`, `sfp`, `rx-internal-delay-ps`, `tx-internal-delay-ps`, and 4 more. Top-level required properties are `reg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `$nodename`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `minimum=0`, `maximum=31`, `maxItems=1`, `maximum=15`, `maximum=200`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `resets` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing; `reset-names` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing; `reset-gpios` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing; `sfp` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure. The highest-risk ABI details are PHY `reg` addresses, compatible fallbacks, reset/interrupt timing, RGMII delays, LED definitions, package topology, and vendor tuning flags.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PHY link autonegotiation, EEE, cable diagnostics, interrupt state, reset state, per-port LEDs, and package shared state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Andrew Lunn <andrew@lunn.ch>, Florian Fainelli <f.fainelli@gmail.com>, Heiner Kallweit <hkallweit1@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/leds/common.yaml#`, `/schemas/net/ethernet-connector.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`. Integration points include phylib, phylink, MDIO child nodes, MAC `phy-handle` users, LED subnodes, interrupts, reset GPIOs, regulators, and board-specific strap properties. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/mdio/fwnode_mdio.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/mdio/mdio-realtek-rtl9300.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/mdio/of_mdio.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t-bananapi-m3.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t-cubietruck-plus.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3-nanopi-m1-plus.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3-nanopi-r1.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3-orangepi-plus.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3-orangepi-plus2e.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3-zeropi.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-r40-bananapi-m2-ultra.dts`, and 2 more. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema allows unknown top-level properties. It has 34 top-level properties and 36 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ethernet-phy.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ethernet-phy.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/ethernet-phy.yaml -->

The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,cpm-enet.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,cpm-enet.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,cpm-mdio.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,cpm-mdio.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Freescale CPM MDIO Device`, a MDIO bus/controller binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branchs with 4 tokens: `fsl,pq1-fec-mdio`, `fsl,cpm2-mdio-bitbang`, `fsl,mpc8272ads-mdio-bitbang`, `fsl,mpc8272-mdio-bitbang`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `fsl,mdio-pin`, `fsl,mdc-pin`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors. The highest-risk ABI details are MDIO address cells, child PHY `reg` values, controller register/clock/reset resources, and scan suppression or reset timing properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including MDIO bus registration, PHY discovery/cache state, clause 22/45 transactions, reset sequencing, and link polling/IRQ state.

The schema description says: The NIC functionality in NETC is known as EtherNET Controller (ENETC). ENETC supports virtualization/isolation based on PCIe Single Root IO Virtualization (SR-IOV), advanced QoS with 8 traffic classes and 4 drop resilience levels, and a full range of TSN standards and NIC offload capabilities It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `phy-mode`, `phy-handle`, `fixed-link`, `local-mac-address`, `mac-address`, `nvmem-cells`, `nvmem-cell-names`, `tx-internal-delay-ps`, `rx-internal-delay-ps`, `phy-supply`, `power-domains`, `fsl,num-tx-queues`, `fsl,num-rx-queues`, `fsl,magic-packet`, `fsl,err006687-workaround-present`, `fsl,stop-mode`, `fsl,pps-channel`, `mdio`, `phy-reset-gpios`, `phy-reset-duration`, `phy-reset-active-high`, `phy-reset-post-delay`, `iommus`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=4`, `minItems=2`, `maxItems=5`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `phy-mode` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure; `phy-handle` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure; `fixed-link` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure; `phy-supply` links board power rails through the regulator framework; `power-domains` integrates the device with SoC PM, DMA, or IOMMU topology; `mdio` models MDIO management bus topology; `phy-reset-gpios` describes board GPIO control/status wiring; `iommus` integrates the device with SoC PM, DMA, or IOMMU topology. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Shawn Guo <shawnguo@kernel.org>, Wei Fang <wei.fang@nxp.com>, NXP Linux Team <linux-imx@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `ethernet-controller.yaml#`, `mdio.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/gpio/gpiolib-of.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/freescale/fec_main.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx25.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx27.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx35.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx50.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx51.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx53.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6qdl.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6sl.dtsi`, and 2 more. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 29 top-level properties and 29 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,fec.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,fec.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,fman-dtsec.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,fman-dtsec.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `NXP FMan MAC`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: Each FMan has several MACs, each implementing an Ethernet interface. Earlier versions of FMan used the Datapath Three Speed Ethernet Controller (dTSEC) for 10/100/1000 MBit/s speeds, and the 10-Gigabit Ethernet Media Access Controller (10GEC) for 10 Gbit/s speeds. Later versions of FMan use the Multirate Ethernet Media Access Controller (mEMAC) to handle all speeds. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maximum=64`, `maxItems=1`, `maxItems=2`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=3`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `pcs-handle` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,fman-port.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,fman-port.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Freescale Frame Manager Port Device`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: The Frame Manager (FMan) supports several types of hardware ports: Ethernet receiver (RX) Ethernet transmitter (TX) Offline/Host command (O/H) It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 6 tokens: `fsl,fman-v2-port-oh`, `fsl,fman-v2-port-rx`, `fsl,fman-v2-port-tx`, `fsl,fman-v3-port-oh`, `fsl,fman-v3-port-rx`, `fsl,fman-v3-port-tx`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `cell-index`, `reg`, `fsl,fman-10g-port`, `fsl,fman-best-effort-port`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `cell-index`; required properties collected from nested branches include `cell-index`, `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include no explicit numeric or item limits collected. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Frank Li <Frank.Li@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/freescale/fman/fman_port.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/qoriq-fman3-0-10g-0.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/qoriq-fman3-0-10g-1.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/qoriq-fman3-0-1g-0.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/qoriq-fman3-0-1g-1.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/qoriq-fman3-0-1g-2.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/qoriq-fman3-0-1g-3.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/qoriq-fman3-0-1g-4.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/qoriq-fman3-0-1g-5.dtsi`, and 2 more. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,fman-port.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,fman-port.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,fman-port.yaml -->

`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,fman.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Freescale Frame Manager Device`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: Due to the fact that the FMan is an aggregation of sub-engines (ports, MACs, etc.) the FMan node will have child nodes for each of them. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Numeric and collection constraints include `minItems=1`, `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `dma-coherent` integrates the device with SoC PM, DMA, or IOMMU topology; `#address-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `#size-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,gianfar-mdio.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,gianfar-mdio.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Freescale Gianfar (TSEC) MDIO Device`, a MDIO bus/controller binding. The schema description says: This binding describes the MDIO is a bus to which the PHY devices are connected. For each device that exists on this bus, a child node should be created. Some TSECs are associated with an internal Ten-Bit Interface (TBI) PHY. This PHY is accessed through the local MDIO bus. These buses are defined similarly to the mdio buses, except they are compatible with "fsl,gianfar-tbi". The TBI PHYs underneath them are similar to normal PHYs, but the reg property is considered instructive, rather than descriptive. The reg... It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 7 tokens: `fsl,gianfar-tbi`, `fsl,gianfar-mdio`, `fsl,etsec2-tbi`, `fsl,etsec2-mdio`, `fsl,ucc-mdio`, `gianfar`, `ucc_geth_phy`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `device_type`. Top-level required properties are `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`; required properties collected from nested branches include `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `compatible`, `device_type`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `minItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors. The highest-risk ABI details are MDIO address cells, child PHY `reg` values, controller register/clock/reset resources, and scan suppression or reset timing properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including MDIO bus registration, PHY discovery/cache state, clause 22/45 transactions, reset sequencing, and link polling/IRQ state.

Maintainers listed: J. Neuschäfer <j.ne@posteo.net>. Direct schema dependencies are `mdio.yaml#`. Integration points include the Linux MDIO and phylib frameworks, Ethernet PHY child nodes, MAC `phy-handle` users, clocks, resets, interrupts, and optional GPIO bit-banging. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 18 top-level properties and 18 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,gianfar.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,gianfar.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,qoriq-mc-dpmac.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/fsl,qoriq-mc-dpmac.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `DPAA2 MAC`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: This binding represents the DPAA2 MAC objects found on the fsl-mc bus and located under the 'dpmacs' node for the fsl-mc bus DTS node. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `fsl,qoriq-mc-dpmac`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `pcs-handle`, `phys`. Top-level required properties are `reg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `pcs-handle` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. It has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/intel,dwmac-plat.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/intel,dwmac-plat.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/intel,ixp46x-ptp-timer.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/intel,ixp46x-ptp-timer.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Intel IXP46x PTP Timer (TSYNC)`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: The Intel IXP46x PTP timer is known in the manual as IEEE1588 Hardware Assist and Time Synchronization Hardware Assist TSYNC provides a PTP timer. It exists in the Intel IXP45x and IXP46x XScale SoCs. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `intel,ixp46x-ptp-timer`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `interrupt-names`, `interrupts`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Herve Codina <herve.codina@bootlin.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/pinctrl/pinctrl.yaml#`, `/schemas/pinctrl/pinmux-node.yaml#`, `/schemas/sound/dai-common.yaml`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/wan/framer/pef2256/pef2256.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 10 top-level properties and 13 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/lantiq,pef2256.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/lantiq,pef2256.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/litex,liteeth.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `LiteX LiteETH ethernet device`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: LiteETH is a small footprint and configurable Ethernet core for FPGA based system on chips. The hardware source is Open Source and can be found on at https://github.com/enjoy-digital/liteeth/. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `phy-handle` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure; `mdio` models MDIO management bus topology. `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/loongson,ls1b-gmac.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Loongson-1B Gigabit Ethernet MAC Controller`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: Loongson-1B Gigabit Ethernet MAC Controller is based on Synopsys DesignWare MAC (version 3.50a). Main features - Dual 10/100/1000Mbps GMAC controllers - Full-duplex operation (IEEE 802.3x flow control automatic transmission) - Half-duplex operation (CSMA/CD Protocol and back-pressure support) - RX Checksum Offload - TX Checksum insertion - MII interface - RGMII interface It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

<!-- BEGIN_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,aquantia.yaml -->
# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,aquantia.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,aquantia.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Marvell Aquantia Ethernet PHY`, a Ethernet PHY binding. The schema description says: Marvell Aquantia Ethernet PHY require a firmware to be loaded to actually work. This can be done and is implemented by OEM in 3 different way: - Attached SPI flash directly to the PHY with the firmware. The PHY will self load the firmware in the presence of this configuration. - Read from a dedicated partition on system NAND declared in an NVMEM cell, and loaded to the PHY using its mailbox interface. - Manually provided firmware loaded from a file in the filesystem. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a composed compatible schema with 0 tokens: no explicit compatible constants. Top-level properties are `reg`, `firmware-name`, `marvell,mdi-cfg-order`, `nvmem-cells`, `nvmem-cell-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors. The highest-risk ABI details are PHY `reg` addresses, compatible fallbacks, reset/interrupt timing, RGMII delays, LED definitions, package topology, and vendor tuning flags.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PHY link autonegotiation, EEE, cable diagnostics, interrupt state, reset state, per-port LEDs, and package shared state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Christian Marangi <ansuelsmth@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `ethernet-phy.yaml#`. Integration points include phylib, phylink, MDIO child nodes, MAC `phy-handle` users, LED subnodes, interrupts, reset GPIOs, regulators, and board-specific strap properties. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: no direct in-tree driver match found by compatible scan. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 5 top-level properties and 6 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,aquantia.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,aquantia.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,aquantia.yaml -->

Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 2 top-level properties and 2 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,mvusb.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,mvusb.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,orion-mdio.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,orion-mdio.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Marvell MDIO Ethernet Controller interface`, a MDIO bus/controller binding. The schema description says: The Ethernet controllers of the Marvel Kirkwood, Dove, Orion5x, MV78xx0, Armada 370, Armada XP, Armada 7k and Armada 8k have an identical unit that provides an interface with the MDIO bus. Additionally, Armada 7k and Armada 8k has a second unit which provides an interface with the xMDIO bus. This driver handles these interfaces. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 2 tokens: `marvell,orion-mdio`, `marvell,xmdio`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=4`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs. The highest-risk ABI details are MDIO address cells, child PHY `reg` values, controller register/clock/reset resources, and scan suppression or reset timing properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including MDIO bus registration, PHY discovery/cache state, clause 22/45 transactions, reset sequencing, and link polling/IRQ state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Andrew Lunn <andrew@lunn.ch>. Direct schema dependencies are `mdio.yaml#`. Integration points include the Linux MDIO and phylib frameworks, Ethernet PHY child nodes, MAC `phy-handle` users, clocks, resets, interrupts, and optional GPIO bit-banging. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/marvell/mvmdio.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/marvell/armada-370-xp.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/marvell/armada-375.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/marvell/armada-38x.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/marvell/dove.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/marvell/kirkwood.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/marvell/orion5x.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/marvell/ac5-98dx25xx.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/marvell/armada-37xx.dtsi`, and 1 more. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,orion-mdio.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/marvell,orion-mdio.yaml` against boards using these nodes. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: no direct in-tree driver match found by compatible scan. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 1 top-level properties and 1 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/maxlinear,gpy2xx.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/maxlinear,gpy2xx.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/mctp-i2c-controller.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/mctp-i2c-controller.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MCTP I2C transport`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: An mctp-i2c-controller defines a local MCTP endpoint on an I2C controller. MCTP I2C is specified by DMTF DSP0237. An mctp-i2c-controller must be attached to an I2C adapter which supports slave functionality. I2C busses (either directly or as subordinate mux busses) are attached to the mctp-i2c-controller with a 'mctp-controller' property on each used bus. Each mctp-controller I2C bus will be presented to the host system as a separate MCTP I2C instance. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `mctp-i2c-controller`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `minimum=1073741824`, `maximum=1073741951`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

Maintainers listed: Matt Johnston <matt@codeconstruct.com.au>. Direct schema dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/mdio-mux-multiplexer.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/mdio-mux-multiplexer.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Properties for an MDIO bus multiplexer consumer device`, a MDIO mux binding. The schema description says: This is a special case of MDIO mux when MDIO mux is defined as a consumer of a mux producer device. The mux producer can be of any type like mmio mux producer, gpio mux producer or generic register based mux producer. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses a single `const` with 1 token: `mdio-mux-multiplexer`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `mux-controls`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `mux-controls`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key. The highest-risk ABI details are child bus `reg` selector values, `#address-cells`/`#size-cells`, mux control resources, and inherited MDIO child constraints.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including active mux channel, child bus registration, PHY probe state, and serialized MDIO transaction routing.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Andrew Lunn <andrew@lunn.ch>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/net/mdio-mux.yaml#`. Integration points include the Linux MDIO mux framework, child MDIO buses, GPIO/MMIO/multiplexer controls, PHY discovery, and MAC-to-PHY phandles. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 10 top-level properties and 10 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/mediatek,star-emac.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/mediatek,star-emac.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
