# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/mediatek-dwmac.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/mediatek-dwmac.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MediaTek DWMAC glue layer controller`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: This file documents platform glue layer for stmmac. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 3 branchs with 5 tokens: `mediatek,mt2712-gmac`, `snps,dwmac-4.20a`, `mediatek,mt8195-gmac`, `snps,dwmac-5.10a`, `mediatek,mt8188-gmac`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `power-domains`, `mediatek,pericfg`, `mediatek,tx-delay-ps`, `mediatek,rx-delay-ps`, `mediatek,rmii-rxc`, `mediatek,rmii-clk-from-mac`, `mediatek,txc-inverse`, `mediatek,rxc-inverse`, `mediatek,mac-wol`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `phy-mode`, `mediatek,pericfg`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `interrupt-names`, `interrupts`, `mediatek,pericfg`, `phy-mode`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `minItems=5`, `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `power-domains` integrates the device with SoC PM, DMA, or IOMMU topology. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

Maintainers listed: Biao Huang <biao.huang@mediatek.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `snps,dwmac.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/stmicro/stmmac/dwmac-mediatek.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/stmicro/stmmac/stmmac_platform.c`. ## Risks

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/microchip,lan8650.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/microchip,lan8650.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Microchip LAN8650/1 10BASE-T1S MACPHY Ethernet Controllers`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: The LAN8650/1 combines a Media Access Controller (MAC) and an Ethernet PHY to enable 10BASE‑T1S networks. The Ethernet Media Access Controller (MAC) module implements a 10 Mbps half duplex Ethernet MAC, compatible with the IEEE 802.3 standard and a 10BASE-T1S physical layer transceiver integrated into the LAN8650/1. The communication between the Host and the MAC-PHY is specified in the OPEN Alliance 10BASE-T1x MACPHY Serial Interface (TC6). It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branchs with 2 tokens: `microchip,lan8650`, `microchip,lan8651`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `spi-max-frequency`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `spi-max-frequency`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `spi-max-frequency`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minimum=15000000`, `maximum=25000000`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Parthiban Veerasooran <parthiban.veerasooran@microchip.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/net/ethernet-controller.yaml#`, `/schemas/spi/spi-peripheral-props.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/microchip/lan865x/lan865x.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/microchip,lan8650.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/microchip,lan8650.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/microchip,lan8650.yaml -->

Integration points include DSA or switchdev, phylink, bridge/VLAN/offload paths, MDIO/PCI/platform buses, switch port child nodes, CPU/user ports, and PHY/fixed-link descriptions. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/microchip/sparx5/sparx5_main.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/reset/reset-microchip-sparx5.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/microchip/lan9691.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/microchip/sparx5.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 10 top-level properties and 18 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/microchip,sparx5-switch.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/microchip,sparx5-switch.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/motorcomm,yt8xxx.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/motorcomm,yt8xxx.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MotorComm yt8xxx Ethernet PHY`, a Ethernet PHY binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 2 tokens: `ethernet-phy-id4f51.e91a`, `ethernet-phy-id4f51.e91b`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `rx-internal-delay-ps`, `tx-internal-delay-ps`, `motorcomm,clk-out-frequency-hz`, `motorcomm,keep-pll-enabled`, `motorcomm,auto-sleep-disabled`, `motorcomm,rx-clk-drv-microamp`, `motorcomm,rx-data-drv-microamp`, `motorcomm,tx-clk-adj-enabled`, `motorcomm,tx-clk-10-inverted`, `motorcomm,tx-clk-100-inverted`, `motorcomm,tx-clk-1000-inverted`. Top-level required properties are none declared at the top level; required properties collected from nested branches include none found. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include no explicit numeric or item limits collected. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key. The highest-risk ABI details are PHY `reg` addresses, compatible fallbacks, reset/interrupt timing, RGMII delays, LED definitions, package topology, and vendor tuning flags.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including PHY link autonegotiation, EEE, cable diagnostics, interrupt state, reset state, per-port LEDs, and package shared state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Frank Sae <frank.sae@motor-comm.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `ethernet-phy.yaml#`. Integration points include phylib, phylink, MDIO child nodes, MAC `phy-handle` users, LED subnodes, interrupts, reset GPIOs, regulators, and board-specific strap properties. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: no direct in-tree driver match found by compatible scan. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mm-kontron-bl-osm-s.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mp-kontron-bl-osm-s.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mp-kontron-smarc.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx93-kontron-bl-osm-s.dts`. The file includes 2 embedded examples for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 12 top-level properties and 12 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/motorcomm,yt8xxx.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/motorcomm,yt8xxx.yaml` against boards using these nodes. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nfc/marvell,nci.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Marvell International Ltd. NCI NFC controller`, a NFC controller binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 3 tokens: `marvell,nfc-i2c`, `marvell,nfc-spi`, `marvell,nfc-uart`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `hci-muxed`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `reset-n-io`, `i2c-int-falling`, `i2c-int-rising`, `break-control`, `flow-control`, `spi-cpha`, `spi-cpol`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`; required properties collected from nested branches include `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors. The highest-risk ABI details are transport address, enable/firmware GPIO polarity, IRQ wiring, clock requirements, and NCI/PN5xx/ST controller compatible matching.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including NFC device registration, firmware download mode, secure-element routing, IRQ-driven frame handling, and power sequencing.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Krzysztof Kozlowski <krzk@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/spi/spi-peripheral-props.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`. Integration points include the Linux NFC/NCI stack, I2C/SPI/UART transport drivers, enable and firmware GPIOs, interrupts, clocks, and regulator supplies. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/gpio/gpiolib-of.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/nfc/nfcmrvl/i2c.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/nfc/nfcmrvl/spi.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/nfc/nfcmrvl/uart.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 3 embedded examples for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 11 top-level properties and 12 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nfc/marvell,nci.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nfc/marvell,nci.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `en-gpios` describes board GPIO control/status wiring; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `wake-gpios` describes board GPIO control/status wiring; `s3fwrn5,en-gpios` describes board GPIO control/status wiring; `s3fwrn5,fw-gpios` describes board GPIO control/status wiring. The highest-risk ABI details are transport address, enable/firmware GPIO polarity, IRQ wiring, clock requirements, and NCI/PN5xx/ST controller compatible matching.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including NFC device registration, firmware download mode, secure-element routing, IRQ-driven frame handling, and power sequencing.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Krzysztof Kozlowski <krzk@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Integration points include the Linux NFC/NCI stack, I2C/SPI/UART transport drivers, enable and firmware GPIOs, interrupts, clocks, and regulator supplies. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/gpio/gpiolib-of.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/nfc/s3fwrn5/i2c.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/nfc/s3fwrn5/uart.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/exynos/exynos5433-tm2-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8916-samsung-a2015-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8916-samsung-fortuna-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8916-samsung-gprimeltecan.dts`. The file includes 2 embedded examples for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 8 top-level properties and 8 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nfc/samsung,s3fwrn5.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nfc/samsung,s3fwrn5.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nfc/samsung,s3fwrn5.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nuvoton,ma35d1-dwmac.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nuvoton,ma35d1-dwmac.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Nuvoton DWMAC glue layer controller`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: Nuvoton 10/100/1000Mbps Gigabit Ethernet MAC Controller is based on Synopsys DesignWare MAC (version 3.73a). It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an ordered fallback `items` sequence with 2 tokens: `nuvoton,ma35d1-dwmac`, `snps,dwmac-3.70a`. Top-level required properties are `clocks`, `clock-names`, `nuvoton,sys`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `phy-mode`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `nuvoton,sys`, `phy-mode`, `reset-names`, `resets`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minimum=0`, `maximum=2000`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `resets` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing; `reset-names` defines reset-controller or GPIO reset sequencing; `phy-mode` connects the MAC/switch port to PHY, PCS, SFP, or fixed-link infrastructure. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates `allOf`, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Joey Lu <yclu4@nuvoton.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `snps,dwmac.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/stmicro/stmmac/dwmac-generic.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/stmicro/stmmac/dwmac-nuvoton.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/stmicro/stmmac/stmmac_platform.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/abilis_tb10x.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8b.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/intel/socfpga/socfpga.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/amlogic/amlogic-c3.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/amlogic/meson-axg.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/amlogic/meson-g12-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/amlogic/meson-gx.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/amlogic/meson-s4.dtsi`, and 2 more. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 11 top-level properties and 11 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nuvoton,ma35d1-dwmac.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nuvoton,ma35d1-dwmac.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/stmicro/stmmac/dwmac-generic.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/stmicro/stmmac/dwmac-lpc18xx.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/stmicro/stmmac/stmmac_platform.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/abilis_tb10x.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/axs10x_mb.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/hsdk.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/vdk_axs10x_mb.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun6i-a31.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun7i-a20-lamobo-r1.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun7i-a20.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t.dtsi`, and 2 more. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. It has 8 top-level properties and 8 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nxp,lpc1850-dwmac.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nxp,lpc1850-dwmac.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nxp,netc-blk-ctrl.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nxp,netc-blk-ctrl.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `NETC Blocks Control`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: Usually, NETC has 2 blocks of 64KB registers, integrated endpoint register block (IERB) and privileged register block (PRB). IERB is used for pre-boot initialization for all NETC devices, such as ENETC, Timer, EMIDO and so on. And PRB controls global reset and global error handling for NETC. Moreover, for the i.MX platform, there is also a NETCMIX block for link configuration, such as MII protocol, PCS protocol, etc. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and referenced common schemas. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 2 tokens: `nxp,imx94-netc-blk-ctrl`, `nxp,imx95-netc-blk-ctrl`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `ranges`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `power-domains`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `ranges`; required properties collected from nested branches include `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `compatible`, `ranges`, `reg`, `reg-names`. Patterned child schemas are `^pcie@[0-9a-f]+$`. Numeric and collection constraints include `maxItems=3`, `maxItems=1`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `#address-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `#size-cells` fixes child-node or phandle specifier shape; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `power-domains` integrates the device with SoC PM, DMA, or IOMMU topology. The highest-risk ABI details are compatible fallback ordering, register/interrupt ordering, clock/reset names, DMA/coherency flags, PHY or fixed-link connection modelling, and vendor resource properties.

## Control Flow
Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` entries, applies `properties`, evaluates no top-level conditionals, checks required fields and min/max constraints, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At runtime the YAML does not execute: a compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then netdev, phylib/phylink, MDIO, DSA/switchdev, NFC, IEEE 802.15.4, regulator, clock, reset, GPIO, IRQ, DMA, IOMMU, and platform-bus helpers consume the validated resources. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Wei Fang <wei.fang@nxp.com>, Clark Wang <xiaoning.wang@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/pci/host-generic-pci.yaml#`. Integration points include Linux netdev Ethernet MAC drivers, phylink/phylib, MDIO buses, DMA/NAPI paths, clocks, resets, power domains, interrupts, IOMMUs, and board DTS MAC nodes. Driver integration is through OF `compatible` matching and subsystem helpers; board integration is through DTS users that instantiate the compatible strings and phandle topology. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/freescale/enetc/netc_blk_ctrl.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx94.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx95.dtsi`. The file includes 1 embedded example for dt-schema validation.

## Risks
Primary risks are Devicetree ABI drift, incomplete compatible coverage, wrong fallback ordering, and resource shape changes that still compile but break driver probe or link bring-up. Tightening required properties can reject existing board files; loosening them can hide missing clocks, resets, interrupts, PHY links, port nodes, or MDIO addresses. Child-node schemas need special care because port numbering, PHY package topology, MDIO mux selectors, NFC transport GPIOs, and switch CPU/user-port layout must match driver tables. This schema rejects unknown top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. It has 9 top-level properties and 9 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be checked against all compatibles and DTS users, not just the filename's nominal device.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nxp,netc-blk-ctrl.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nxp,netc-blk-ctrl.yaml` against boards using these nodes. High-value negative checks remove required `reg`, interrupts, clocks, resets, PHY links, MDIO children, switch ports, or GPIOs; use invalid child `reg` values; misspell vendor properties; and exercise every compatible-specific branch. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, correct netdev/PHY/MDIO/DSA/NFC/802.15.4 registration, clean link or controller bring-up, valid interrupt/reset/power sequencing, and no dtbs_check regressions in embedded examples or in-tree DTS users.

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/nxp,s32-dwmac.yaml` defines the Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `NXP S32G2xx/S32G3xx/S32R45 GMAC ethernet controller`, a Ethernet MAC/controller binding. The schema description says: This device is a Synopsys DWC IP, integrated on NXP S32G/R SoCs. The SoC series S32G2xx and S32G3xx feature one DWMAC instance, the SoC S32R45 has two instances. The devices can use RGMII/RMII/MII interface over Pinctrl device or the output can be routed to the embedded SerDes for SGMII connectivity. The DWMAC instances have connected all RX/TX queues interrupts, enabling load balancing of data traffic across all CPU cores. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable networking code. Its purpose is to keep DTS/DTB nodes aligned with the resources and topology expected by the matching kernel subsystem before drivers probe hardware.

Top-level required properties are `clocks`, `clock-names`; required properties collected from nested branches include `clock-names`, `clocks`. Patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and collection constraints include `minItems=1`, `maxItems=11`. Important resource roles: `compatible` is the stable hardware ABI and OF match key; `reg` describes MMIO ranges, MDIO child addresses, SPI/I2C addresses, or port selectors; `interrupts` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes link/status, RX/TX, error, or management IRQs; `clocks` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs; `clock-names` binds MAC/MDIO/PHY/PTP/SerDes clock inputs. Child-node control flow is declarative too: MDIO children, switch ports, PHY packages, connector/LED nodes, and graph/fixed-link nodes are discovered by common Devicetree helpers according to the schema shape.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file stores no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistence is ABI stability: compatible strings, required resource names, phandle/cell counts, child-node names, port IDs, PHY addresses, fallback ordering, and example layouts become long-lived contracts in DTS and DTB artifacts. Runtime state belongs to drivers and hardware after probe, including netdev registration, DMA rings, NAPI scheduling, runtime PM, link mode negotiation through phylink/phylib, MAC address assignment, and PTP/TSN state where supported.
